#### F13: F13: Make evidence completeness explicit (Human and model review views silently omit artifact content)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT: both halves hold. Minor overstatements (JSON over 16 KB is uncommon, U+FFFD is cosmetic, the write editor holds the full initialText) do not change the verdict; severity P2 (review P2). No stored data is lost, but decisions (TELLS compare, C7 step rows, GREAT ranks that gate loops) can be made on partial evidence without notice. (Verification 1288-1296 (cluster root cause), 1331-1367 (F13), 1370-1460 (options), 1462-1472 (recommendation), 1513-1556 (Change 2 and Change 3 design), 1582-1598 (steps 8-12), 1607-1613 (acceptance tests), 1616-1626 (compatibility), 1647-1657 (ADR-B, ADR-C), 1660-1672 (not doing), 1683-1685 (how it resolves F13), 1688-1726 (adversarial rounds and final-check residuals); section 1 row at line 40)
- **Root cause:** A representation built for a lighter context is reused in a decision context and cannot say it is partial. (a) ArtifactInfo had one overloaded 'text' field: a byte prefix built for step rows and contact sheets, with nothing marking it as a prefix, so decision views rendered it as the artifact and row summaries counted from it. (b) BLIND-1 cards assumed an item's words fit one 2,576 px tile: fit was estimated, not measured; overflow was clipped silently by CSS; and in cards form the card was the only carrier of the words, because the text slot held CARD_NOTE.
- **Wider than reviewed:** StepThumbs.tsx:57-67 counted a diff's files and +/- lines from the prefix (54 KB 12-file diff read '4 files, +142 −142'). PickPanel non-page drafts had no path to the whole text. Further decision surfaces: ComparePanel, StepDetail, RunStatusPanel. cardOf kept tiles[0] only (critic.ts:185-186). The cards-form text slot held only CARD_NOTE (critic.ts:247). cards.test.ts:74 pinned the clipped render. The spec sanctioned excerpts only for step rows (product-v1-design.md:158). A GREAT rank, which gates loops, could be computed on clipped text, ours included.
- **Refuted or overstated:** Minor overstatements only (above). From the review's repair, the 'source artifact' and 'transformation policy' fields were declined as having no consumer, and multi-image pagination was declined because it breaks BLIND-1's one image per label and is still budget-bound (verification 1373-1391, 1660-1665).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** (a) ArtifactInfo (apps/web/src/view/rundata.ts:13-29) is now {mediaType, excerpt?, complete?, bytes?, diffstat?, titles?}. artifactInfo (apps/web/src/server/run-data.ts) streaming-decodes the first 16,000 bytes, so it never ends in half a character, and computes diffstat and option titles from the whole bytes. The rename forced every consumer to choose a snippet, the whole text or a server fact, and a guard scan in evidence.test.ts limits who reads or sets .excerpt (3584a41 extends it to destructuring and element access). Large previews (ArtifactPreview size='large', used by Compare, References, StepDetail and RunStatusPanel) call useWholeText → loadWholeText, which keeps one promise per content-addressed id and evicts failures. Until the text arrives they show PartialNotice with sizes that always differ and an openHref link. TextPreview parses JSON only from whole text, StepThumbs reads diffstat and titles, PickPanel and ComparePanel give every option an Open. (b) cardHtml has no max-height or overflow:hidden. fitCard measures every render and fails closed ('too-tall'/'empty' → StepError naming the item, never tiles[0]). Text critics' cards carry a fixed line and the full words go in the view under each label (critic.ts buildLineup), rendered once with cut:false. Image-only critics get a measured cut with a visible '[… N more words not shown]' line, a card2/ JSON memo with the cut, Lineup.cut by item, and a cutNote appended to the step note (e934f6b; ADR-0090). 88f0a77 makes the fit search converge. Mutations in my clone confirm the tests catch the causes returning. The old byte-prefix decode with diffstat/titles from the prefix fails 3 evidence.test cases. Keeping the first tile plus a CARD_NOTE-only text view fails 6 cards/critic tests. Removing the note push fails the step-note test. One limit stays by design: for image-only critics (C5 gate, web-page) words beyond one tile at 12 px still do not reach the critic. That cut is measured, visible and disclosed, not silent, and the verification rated this branch 'partly'.
- **Records:** ADR-0086 (human half), ADR-0087 (model half), ADR-0090 lines 57-58 (card-cut note reaches the step; the card fit converges), ADR-0100 item 8 (whole-text loader fetches the raw address) and item 9 (current ADR-0087 measurement table), apps/web/README.md excerpt bullet (line 111 on), docs/backlog.md 'Remember run-data row facts by artifact id', docs/backlog.md 'An admission-time warning for long examples in image-only recipes', docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F13, Verification Cluster C, Changes 2 and 3
- **Tests:** apps/web/test/evidence.test.ts: 'marks a long text as an excerpt with its size...'; 'cuts the excerpt on a character boundary, never inside one'; 'counts a diff's files and lines from the whole diff, not the excerpt'; 'names an option map's options from the whole JSON'; 'a diff row counts the whole diff'; 'a large preview of an excerpt says so, and links to the whole text...'; 'the partial notice states sizes that differ...'; 'never shows a partial notice for a text that is all there...'; 'the whole text is shown to its last words'; 'JSON is read only from the whole text...'; 'a diff snippet does not state the cut file's counts as if they were whole'; 'fetches each artifact once, and fetches again after a failure'; 'who reads or sets an excerpt (F13)' scan and fixtures; apps/web/test/format.test.ts partShown cases; apps/web/test/open-links.test.ts: PickPanel, ComparePanel and References Open for text and pages; apps/web/e2e/artifact-views.spec.ts: 'a compare of two long memos that differ only in their endings shows both endings'; 'a long memo is whole in its step and in the viewer; a big diff's row counts every file' ('12 files, +480 −480'); packages/runner/test/cards.test.ts: 'never clip...'; the fitting-a-card cases (wide openings, floor try, character cut, drop all then fail, 'a card whose words reach the critic another way is rendered once and never cut'); Chromium cases (20k-30k characters, capitals, CJK, 'a media lineup records whose words its cards cut'); packages/runner/test/critic.test.ts: 'a critic that reads text gets every item's whole words under its label...'; 'whether a label carries words follows whether its item has words, never whether it is ours'; 'an image-only critic's card cuts words visibly...'; 'a cut is kept with its card...'; 'the note names the cut items once over the step's shuffles...'; 'says in the step's note whose words the critic was not shown'; 'a card that does not fit in one picture stops the step...'; In my clone at 8401820: the 5 web unit files (96 tests) and critic+cards (47 tests, Chromium cases ran) pass. I did not run the seeded artifact-views e2e (needs the demo server and Temporal)

#### F14: F14 — Recover the recommendation state after a successful retry

- **Verification:** ACCEPT; severity P2 (review P2), low-end: the in-app Settings path remounts and works (verification:1751). (1745-1754 (F14), 1731 (cluster root cause rule b), 1789 (option B part 3), 1904-1923 (design slice 2), 1933-1935 (steps 7-9), 1953-1957 (acceptance), 1961-1964 and 1971 (compatibility), 1981-1983 and 1988-1989 (not doing), 1996 (how it resolves F14), 2001, 2005, 2006, 2018, 2019 (adversarial changes), 2029 and 2033 (final-check residuals); verdict table line 41)
- **Root cause:** The screen has no rule for which server state it owns and how that state changes. The recommendation, and a selection derived from it with an invented 'c4' fallback, are copied into useState once at mount, while the screen-mode gate and remainingUsd read live props. The only re-read path, router.refresh(), re-runs a server render that is not a pure read: it makes the first recommendation (F03). So a 'refresh the live props' step can both put owned state out of step with the props and trigger a model call nobody asked for. Recovery is a props refresh, not a state transition, so the gate and the body disagree and a client-made default is shown as the person's choice (verification:1753).
- **Wider than reviewed:** A composed recipe built while the failure panel is up is hidden, because the gate ignores selection.kind === 'composed'. remainingUsd is server-derived and kept current only by router.refresh(). router.refresh() is itself unsafe here: page.tsx:48 asks the model while no recommendation is stored, and a probe showed a re-render after compose during a failure adds one advisor.recommend call. The 'c4' default is tagged 'Your choice' (tag logic at :114). The examples are lost on the Settings detour (shared with F06) (verification:1749, 2001, 2006).
- **Refuted or overstated:** None. The refuter's caveats (the in-app Settings, Save, Back path remounts and works; the realistic triggers are transient model errors) lower the frequency but do not refute the defect (verification:1749).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** RecommendationView now owns one RecState, seeded once by initialRecState (selection = chosenRecipeId ?? rec?.recipeId ?? null, so no 'c4') and never re-seeded from props. Try again POSTs {ensure:true}, and recommendCommand calls currentRecommendation, which returns the stored recommendation or makes and stores one. flip and compose are commands too. Each returns {recommendation|composed, remainingUsd}, with the remaining amount read after the command (api.ts:203-216), and is applied by recovered, rerouted or composedReady in one setState. RecommendationView no longer calls router.refresh(): grep finds it only in SettingsView and SpendingRecords, whose renders are pure reads. screen() shows the failure panel only when nothing else can be shown. 'Your choice' appears only for an alternative the person picked. page.tsx:57-58 keys the view by `${intakeId}:${chosenRecipeId}`. The impure first-recommendation GET render itself remains (F03): concurrent first loads are deduped by ADR-0091 and ADR-0099, and moving it off the render is filed in the backlog.
- **Records:** ADR-0082 (Decision R2, Recommendation bullet; Consequences), ADR-0091 (refers to {ensure:true} and dedupes duplicate first recommendations, F03), spec §5 Recommendation line, docs/backlog.md 'Move the first recommendation off the Recommendation page's GET render', status page row F14: Fixed, 0082
- **Tests:** apps/web/test/recommendation.test.ts 'the Recommendation screen's state (F14)' (in my clone, reinstating a 'c4' default made 'starts with nothing selected … no made-up default' and 'a failed retry keeps the failure panel' fail); apps/web/test/api.test.ts 'Recommendation screen commands return everything they change (F14, ADR-0082)': ensure makes and stores the recommendation once (advisor called once across repeats); 'Build my own during a failure asks the model for no recommendation'; the raised cap is reflected; RecommendInput accepts exactly {ensure:true} or {answers} (5 of 5 passed in my clone); apps/web/test/api-client.test.ts 'post() and patch() fail … when a successful response has a body that cannot be read'; apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts:85 'F14: after a refused recommendation, Try again shows the stored recommendation with its reasons, and Start sends it' (failed second retry; title, Why this recipe, How byway decided, raised remaining figure, a flip that re-routes, Start POST recipeId); recovery.spec.ts:127 'F14: Build my own while the recommendation is refused … asks the model nothing behind your back' (no RSC request for the /recommend path; Try again keeps the composed selection); state.spec.ts:387 (examples survive the Settings detour); Both recovery cases passed in CI run 39177268735 at 8401820 on Linux and Windows (the 'recovery' project runs last, after 'chromium')

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| After a successful Try again the screen still shows Memo Polish / Your choice with no chooser; a reload shows the stored recipe | fixed at root | recovered() applies the command result in one transition; recovery.spec.ts:85 checks #proposal-title 'Landing Page Studio', no 'Your choice', and the Why and How headings, without a reload |
| useState(initial) copies the recommendation once; selection falls back to 'c4' | fixed | initialRecState has no fallback (recommendation.ts:39-42); grep finds no 'c4' default in src/app; the unit test is sensitive to the mutation (checked in my clone) |

#### F15: F15 — Do not regress run state when polls arrive out of order

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity P3 (review P2): the display regression heals within one poll interval, nothing wrong can be persisted, and the meter is already monotone; the lasting harms are a sticky false-offline banner on ended runs and, through F06, a lost draft (verification:1762). (1756-1765 (F15), 1731 (cluster root rule b and c), 1770-1784 (option A), 1787-1808 (option B), 1844 (why B), 1851-1865 (design), 1928-1929 (steps 2-3), 1937 (backlog asks), 1949-1952 (acceptance), 1977 and 1987 (not doing), 1997 (how it resolves F15), 2007, 2009, 2012, 2013, 2022 (adversarial changes), 2028 (stalled-request residual); verdict table line 42)
- **Root cause:** Server snapshots reach the client (RunScreen, and Nav's waiting count in the same way) through several overlapping, uncancellable requests with no single owner of the request sequence. Each response is applied, and sets the connection state, in arrival order. mergeRunData is right to treat a snapshot as authoritative; what is missing is control over which snapshot reaches it (verification:1764).
- **Wider than reviewed:** A late failure after a newer success sets 'lost' (RunScreen.tsx:72-74 at b33f128), and pollDelay returns null for ended runs, so a permanent false 'byway is not answering' banner sticks on a finished run. Nav's waiting count polls the same way. Event seq cannot serve as a revision: view() reads the run, then events and checkpoints, separately, and the runner writes state before its event. Torn reads within one response persist: cancelRun, failRun and settleLostRun write the terminal status before withdrawing open questions, so a snapshot can read 'cancelled' with a question still open, after which polling stops. A stale snapshot unmounts a just-opened panel and wipes typed text (F06). Large open-checkpoint artifacts re-hashed on every poll widen the reorder window (verification:1760-1762, 1937).
- **Refuted or overstated:** Visible spend cannot go down: meter.ts runSpend takes the max of the run record and the latest cost event (still true on main, meter.ts:35-42). A revived terminal run lasts at most one poll interval, because the regressed status re-arms polling and the next snapshot heals it; server fencing (runner client.ts, the api.ts resolveInRun 409s) refuses answers and controls on stale state. The refuter's 'cleared by the next poll' was wrong for ended runs (verification:1760).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** Each screen now has one owner of its request sequence. createRefresher (view/refresh.ts) bumps a generation and aborts the previous request on now(); poll() joins the in-flight request. Only a request that is still the latest, not disposed and not aborted may apply data or set the connection state. A request this instance aborted never sets 'lost'. A request unanswered after 30 s (body included) counts as lost and applies nothing. useRefresher creates and disposes one instance per key (runId for RunScreen, pathname for Nav) and re-reads the server-rendered snapshot through the same path. usePolling re-arms only after a poll settles and skips hidden tabs. RunScreen polls at LOST_RETRY_MS while the connection is lost, whatever the status, so the banner clears on ended runs. mergeRunData is unchanged: with ordered apply, 'latest snapshot wins' holds, because each applied snapshot was read after the previous applied one arrived. R3 (answerable = open AND not ended) guards the one tear that can stick. get(), post() and patch() treat a successful status with an unreadable body as a failure. The review's server snapshot revision and consistent view() reads were deliberately not built (ADR-0082) and are filed with triggers.
- **Records:** ADR-0082 (Decision R2 polling bullet, R3, 'No server snapshot revision is added for now … Revisit triggers'; Consequences), docs/backlog.md 'A server snapshot revision and consistent view() reads', docs/backlog.md 'F15 e2e (b): land the one failure on the post-answer refresh only', status page row F15: Fixed, 0082
- **Tests:** apps/web/test/refresh.test.ts 'refresh sequencing (F15)': 'applies only the newer snapshot when an older one arrives after it', 'a late failure of an older request leaves the connection ok', 'aborts the older request … never sets the connection state', 'joins an in-flight request instead of starting another poll', 'gives up on a request that never answers', 'a request whose answer comes only after its timeout is lost, never applied', 'a new instance for the same run applies normally (StrictMode remount)'; Mutation checks in my clone. Reverting to arrival order (removing both the abort and the success-path generation guard) fails 2 tests. Making poll() not single-flight fails 1. Removing the error-path latest guard fails 4. Removing the success-path generation guard alone survives, because the harness fetch, like a real one, rejects on abort, so the abort check already blocks a superseded answer; apps/web/test/api-client.test.ts (get() fails on a body cut off by its signal or not JSON); apps/web/test/drafts.test.ts (answerable with a torn 'cancelled' + open question); apps/web/e2e/state.spec.ts:77 'a late, older poll never reopens an answered question, and a late failure leaves no banner on a finished run' (cases a, b, c and the late failure after Done), passed in CI run 39177268735 at 8401820 on Linux (18.6 s) and Windows (43.3 s)

| Claim | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| An older poll after a newer one regresses done to waiting and resolved to open | fixed at root (upstream of the merge) | Only the latest-issued request applies (refresh.ts:57-90); e2e state.spec.ts:77 case (a) holds an older GET, releases it after the answer and checks the answered question never returns for 2.5 s |
| RunScreen starts timer, visibility, answer and control refreshes without awaiting | fixed | usePolling re-arms after settle; visibilitychange calls poll() (joins); resolving and controlling await refresher.now() (RunScreen.tsx:142, 155) |
| rundata.ts replaces run and checkpoints wholesale in arrival order | kept by design; order now enforced before it | mergeRunData unchanged (rundata.ts); ADR-0082: its 'latest snapshot wins' is now true |
| WS-02: cost $0.20 to $0.10 / acceptance 'cannot lower spend' | refuted for the visible meter | meter.ts:35-42 runSpend takes the max of the run record and the latest cost event (verification:1760) |
| Root fix: a monotonic server snapshot revision covering run and checkpoints | not built, deliberately; filed with trigger | ADR-0082 'No server snapshot revision is added for now'; backlog 'A server snapshot revision and consistent view() reads': revisit on push/SSE, state shared across tabs, or a tear R3 does not cover |
| Root fix: single-flight, or cancel obsolete requests including on unmount and run change | built | refresh.ts poll() and now(); useRefresher disposes on key change and unmount; RunScreen is keyed by run id (app/runs/[id]/page.tsx:24) |
| Keep event dedupe; do not use event seq as a revision | followed | mergeRunData dedupes by seq; no seq-based ordering was introduced (verification:1823, 1760) |
| Consider snapshot-consistent reads | not built; filed with trigger | runner client.ts:190-195 still reads the run and then events and checkpoints separately; lost-runs.ts:52-57 still writes the terminal status before withdrawing; R3 guards; same backlog row |
| Acceptance: cannot reopen a resolved checkpoint or revive a terminal run | tested | state.spec.ts:77 (a) and the late failure after Done ('.run-status' stays 'Done') |
| Acceptance: a late fetch error must not overwrite a newer success | fixed and tested | refresh.test 'a late failure of an older request leaves the connection ok'; e2e: no banner for 5 s after the held request is aborted post-Done |
| Acceptance: server fencing stays intact | unchanged | ADR-0082 Consequences: 'Server fencing (409s on stale answers) is unchanged' |
| Wider: a permanent false 'not answering' banner on ended runs | fixed | Status is set only by the latest request; RunScreen.tsx:101 polls at LOST_RETRY_MS while lost whatever the status; state.spec.ts:77 case (b) shows the banner, then it clears |
| Wider: Nav's waiting count polls in arrival order | fixed; no Nav-specific test | Nav.tsx:33-39 uses useRefresher and usePolling; covered only by the shared refresh.test |
| Wider: a torn read (terminal status written before withdrawal) | guarded client-side (R3); filed | answerable() in RunScreen.tsx:82 and drafts.settle; drafts.test torn-read case; backlog row trigger 'a tear R3 does not cover' |
| Wider: a stale snapshot unmounts the panel and wipes the draft (with F06) | fixed | Closed twice: ordered apply, and the F06 draft store above the slot |
| Wider: per-poll re-read and re-hash of open-checkpoint artifacts widens the reorder window | partly filed | getRunData still re-reads open-checkpoint artifacts each poll and store.artifacts.get re-hashes them (packages/store/src/artifacts.ts:92-105). The nearest row is 'Remember run-data row facts by artifact id' (ADR-0086; trigger 'only if the cost shows'), which covers re-parsing, not the re-read and re-hash verification step 11 asked to file. With ordered apply, the wider window is now harmless, so this is efficiency only |
| Final-check residual: a stalled GET stops all polling with no banner | fixed | REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS 30 s in refresh.ts; refresh.test 'gives up on a request that never answers' |
| Re-check: a body cut off by the timeout was applied as an empty snapshot and crashed the screen | fixed test-first | 282d544; refresh.test 'a request whose answer comes only after its timeout is lost'; api-client.test |
| Overstated: a revived terminal run persists | refuted (at most one poll interval) | verification:1760 |

| What remains | Filed as | Trigger | OK |
|---|---|---|---|
| No storage-level snapshot revision and no single-transaction view() reads; a torn 'ended run with an open question' can still be served | backlog 'A server snapshot revision and consistent view() reads' (ADR-0082) | push or server-sent events, state shared across tabs, or a tear R3 does not cover | yes |
| F15 e2e (b) can still fail in a narrow race (a timer poll takes the 503 just as the post-answer refresh supersedes it) | backlog 'F15 e2e (b): land the one failure on the post-answer refresh only' | none stated (a test-only flake, rare) | **no** |
| Open-checkpoint artifacts are re-read and re-hashed on every poll | approximately: backlog 'Remember run-data row facts by artifact id' (ADR-0086) | 'only if the cost shows' | yes |

**Gaps:**

- Status page row F15 says 'What remains: —', but the server snapshot revision and consistent reads (the review's own root fix, deliberately not built) are a backlog row with triggers, and an e2e race is filed. The column could name them, as other rows do.
- Verification step 11 asked for a backlog row on per-poll re-reading and re-hashing of open-checkpoint artifacts. No row with that scope was added; the ADR-0086 row covers only re-parsing of row facts.
- The backlog row for the F15 e2e (b) race has no trigger.
- Nav's use of the refresher has no test of its own.
- Minor test sensitivity: the success-path generation guard in refresh.ts is not tested independently of the abort check. Removing it alone leaves refresh.test green, because aborted fetches reject. Ordering still holds through the abort; arrival-order application returning (both removed) is caught.

#### F16: F16: Sanitize successful gateway results before persistence (summary table: 'Secret sanitation protects evidence copies but not outgoing results'; sub-review gateway-store-review.md GS-05)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity P3 (review P2). (Verdict row l.43; §2 table l.67; §3 l.82; cluster G l.3417-3457 (F16 l.3433-3457); options l.3488-3572; recommendation and design l.3574-3768; per-finding resolution l.3772-3777; adversarial changes l.3788-3805; final check and residuals l.3808-3817)
- **Root cause:** Nothing enforces the key promise where a key can cross a boundary. The only secret mechanism, makeRedactor, ran only on the gateway's own diagnostic copies (wire bodies, error strings). Four crossings were unchecked: outbound request text before dispatch; inbound completion text before it reaches runner, advisor and memos; inbound provider metadata (servedModel, upstreamProvider) before ledger and results; user material at the browser-to-server boundary before it is stored and shown. redact.ts:22-23, ADR-0074 and spec §2 claimed more than the code did (verification lines 3452-3457, cluster statement 3419-3430).
- **Wider than reviewed:** (1) The leak starts at input, which the review missed: a pasted key is stored unredacted (api.ts createIntake, refs.ts storeRef) and sent to the provider as-is (gateway.ts passes messages through), so an output filter alone cannot make ADR-0074 or spec §2 true (l.3444). (2) The ledger's servedModel and upstreamProvider were written unredacted (l.3440). (3) The redact.ts comment 'applied to every outbound string' overclaimed (l.3457). (4) The design adds: an ASSET step must stop on a held-key image refusal, and modelProblem must show the gateway's message (l.3605-3607). (5) The final check found that makeRedactor did not scrub a bare FAL_KEY secret half that findHeldKey matches (l.3811).
- **Refuted or overstated:** (a) Error paths were already redacted (errors.ts:70, gateway.ts:239/300/607), so that part of the root fix was already true (l.3445). (b) Shape-based redaction (KEY_SHAPES) of product text is rejected: it would rewrite legitimate text, for example a quoted 'sk-proj-...' placeholder (verifier case C, l.3446). (c) 'Pretending byte-exact' is mostly moot: record() already documented that evidence is stored redacted (l.3447). (d) Severity drops to P3: the precondition is the person's own pasted key, which is already stored and sent before any echo, on the single owner's machine (l.3450).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** All four unchecked crossings named in the verification's root cause are now checked by one exact rule, and each check has a test that fails when it is removed (mutation runs in a scratch clone). (1) Browser to server: parseBody(request, schema, heldKeyIn) runs findHeldKey over every string of the parsed body, property names included (strings-in.ts). It returns a 400 naming the variable before anything is stored (http.ts:41-62). Every route that reads a JSON body passes deps.heldKeyIn: intakes, intakes/[id]/recommend, refs, runs, runs/[id]/control, checkpoints, spend, settings PATCH and renderer PATCH. (2) Gateway out: refuseHeldKeys runs after validation and before profile resolution, reservation, exposure or dispatch, in complete() (system and every text part, gateway.ts:221, requestTexts l.1052), image() (l.331) and video() (l.513). (3) Gateway in: after record() has ledgered the call with redacted evidence, findHeldKey runs on out.text (gateway.ts:304-309). A hit throws a non-retryable provider-error with the heldKey marker, billed and settled, so the text never reaches CompletionResult, memos or artifacts. It also runs on the after-decline retry, since that reuses completeOnce. (4) Metadata: record() normalises labels (providerLabel, F19) and then scrubs them (scrubLabels, gateway.ts:903-910, 1047), and the results return the ledger's scrubbed value. Error paths stay redacted. makeRedactor's comment was corrected, and it now also scrubs each held-key ':'-part (redact.ts:33), which closes the final-check residual. asset.ts:247 rethrows isHeldKeyError, and modelProblem maps held-key errors to 400 and 502. ADR-0088 amends ADR-0074's Keys bullet without editing it; spec §2 Keys (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-product-v1-design.md:42) now says what is enforced and what is not. Follow-ups stop the browser tab keeping a refused paste on Start, examples and run answers (ADR-0100 item 10).
- **Records:** ADR-0088 (Decision 'Held keys', 'What the promise does not cover', Consequences), ADR-0100 items 10 and 11 (correct ADR-0088's session-draft sentence and route count), ADR-0101 Decision 'Held keys' (answers checked on raw text; JSON \u escapes decoded after the check), ADR-0102 (a billed held-key retry keeps its own words), ADR index rows: ADR-0074 and ADR-0069 'changed by ADR-0088'; ADR-0088 'changed by ADR-0100', spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-product-v1-design.md §2 Keys (l.42), §5 Screens 1-3 (l.170-171), backlog 'Start keeps attachments per attachment after a held-key refusal', status page docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F16
- **Tests:** packages/gateway/test/keys.test.ts: findHeldKey (exact match, FAL half alone, ignores non-provider vars, short values, key-shaped strings not held; reads env at call time; never returns value), 'the held-key marker' (cross-bundle duck typing), makeRedactor 'scrubs each :-part of a held key too'; packages/gateway/test/gateway.test.ts 'gateway: held provider keys (ADR-0088)': 'refuses a completion whose %s holds a key, before any reservation, exposure record or call' (user message, system, later text part), 'refuses an image whose prompt holds any held key', 'refuses in demo mode too', 'rejects a billed answer that holds a key: ledgered first, never returned, and not retried', 'returns a clean answer byte for byte, even one quoting a key-shaped string byway does not hold', 'scrubs a key out of the served model and the upstream host...', 'scrubs a key out of an image's served model and keeps the image: nothing is bought twice'; 'never writes a key to the ledger or the stored bodies when a provider echoes one'; packages/gateway/test/video.test.ts 'refuses a clip prompt holding the FAL_KEY's secret half on its own, before any upload, job or reservation (ADR-0088)'; packages/runner/test/memo.test.ts 'an answer that holds a provider key (ADR-0088) > is never memoized: the real gateway refuses it after ledgering, and the failure is not retried'; packages/runner/test/activities.test.ts 'an image refused because its prompt holds a provider key (ADR-0088) > stops the step with the gateway's message...' and '...still tries any other refused image again'; apps/web/test/http.test.ts 'parseBody refuses a body holding a held provider key' (value, attachment, attachment name, record property name; field naming; accepts key-shaped text; checks what the schema keeps); apps/web/test/services.test.ts 'finds a held provider key anywhere in a request body, a FAL_KEY by its secret half alone...'; apps/web/test/api.test.ts:341-342 (modelProblem: held-key invalid-request -> 400, provider-error -> 502); apps/web/e2e/pasted-key.spec.ts (5 demo e2e tests: Start text, Start attachment, example you admire, Run answer; no intake stored, watchForKeys leaks empty); Ran in clone C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\byway-fixes\scratch\review-map\G-F16-F17\repo at 8401820: 8 files, 226 tests pass. Mutations (each reverted): out-check removed in complete() -> 4 gateway tests fail; in-check on out.text removed -> 2 fail (gateway + memo); parseBody held check removed -> 13 fail; scrubLabels made a no-op -> 2 fail

**Gaps:**

- Status page row F16 says 'What remains: —'. The backlog row 'Start keeps attachments per attachment after a held-key refusal' (an ADR-0088 follow-up, no trigger) and the recorded limits (images, pre-change data, \u-escaped JSON per ADR-0101) are not mentioned. The Records cell names only ADR-0088, although ADR-0100 items 10-11 correct it.
- The limits are Delegated (ADR-0088: 'not explicitly approved'), so none of them is an owner-accepted limit.
- No guard test stops a future body-reading route from bypassing parseBody.

#### F17: F17: Restrict media retrieval and enforce limits while streaming (summary table: 'Provider media fetch trusts destinations and buffers before checking size'; sub-review gateway-store-review.md GS-06)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity P3, top of P3 (review P2). (Verdict row l.44; §2 table l.67; §3 l.82; cluster G l.3417-3431; F17 l.3460-3485; options l.3488-3572; recommendation and design l.3574-3768 (media step 5 l.3617-3639; tests step 6 l.3668-3676); resolution l.3778-3785; adversarial changes l.3789-3803; final check l.3808-3817)
- **Root cause:** download() in fal.ts:87-98 trusts any URL fal names once its scheme is https, and leaves the rest to fetch's defaults and provider-API conventions: no host, port or userinfo policy, and redirects followed to any host, http downgrade included; a size ceiling only through the optional, encoded-length Content-Length and a check after full buffering; the clip typed from headers; CDN statuses classified as provider-API statuses (401 read as a missing key). The intent in the comments was never implemented, and no ADR recorded how far provider media is trusted (l.3480-3485).
- **Wider than reviewed:** (1) A CDN 401 became 'no-credentials', so relay.ts skipped the link with 'No video provider key is configured' after the clip was billed, and a 404 became 'invalid-request' (critic probe401, l.3476, 3793). (2) The clip was typed from headers or fal's content_type, never from its bytes (l.3468). (3) The practical vector reaches further than the review says: the redirect downgrade to plain-HTTP loopback and LAN, whose body was stored as a billed video/mp4 (T2b, l.3466, 3473). (4) A gzip body (611 KB on the wire, 600 MiB decoded) was fully buffered (l.3467). (5) Parked to the backlog: the page renderer's RENDER_ALLOWLIST checked only a request's first URL (the same class at another boundary), and asset.ts re-bought billed non-retryable images (l.3693-3695, 3804). (6) Final-check residuals: the live fal host set is unconfirmed; readCapped preallocation would throw a RangeError on an encoded body longer than its declared length (l.3810-3812).
- **Refuted or overstated:** (a) The review's 'reproduced' https://127.0.0.1 used a fake fetch; under real fetch it fails TLS (DEPTH_ZERO_SELF_SIGNED_CERT). The real vector is the redirect downgrade. (b) 'False Content-Length' works only through Content-Encoding; an understated length just truncates (T5). (c) The DNS-to-connect race check is not adopted: once every hop must be TLS-verified HTTPS on fal's media domain, a private service cannot answer for that name (l.3472-3475). Severity drops to P3: the attacker must be fal, its CDN or a MITM with a trusted certificate; the path is live-only; the SSRF is a blind GET (l.3478).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** download() in packages/gateway/src/providers/fal.ts (l.208-251) no longer leaves destination, size, type or status to defaults. admitMediaUrl (l.161-163: https:, empty username and password, port '', hostname matching /^(?:[a-z0-9-]+[.])*fal[.]media$/) runs before the first fetch (l.215) and on every resolved Location (l.231). fetch is called with redirect:'manual' and one AbortSignal.timeout covering every hop (l.216-220). A 3xx without a Location is refused, and more than MAX_REDIRECTS=3 hops are refused. A non-ok media status throws mediaError('provider-error'), retryable only for 408, 429 and 5xx (l.236-239), so a CDN 401 can no longer become no-credentials (relay.ts:90 skips only on no-credentials). A declared length over the cap is refused before reading. readCapped (l.180-198) counts the decoded bytes the reader yields, cancels the body past the cap, and copies into one resizable ArrayBuffer (9ee06e8: about the cap, not twice it; it grows past a too-small declared length, which closes the RangeError residual). sniffVideoType (l.201-205) types the clip from its bytes, so a non-video body is ledgered but not kept. The evidence records media.clip and media.frame origins. The comments (l.142-153) describe what is enforced. ADR-0088 records the media trust policy and extends ADR-0069. The two parked items were fixed later: renderer redirect hops (56e30ff, ADR-0096) and the asset re-buy (4b3f5b6).
- **Records:** ADR-0088 (Decision 'Provider media', Alternatives: IP pinning, deny-list and record-only allowlist rejected; Negative: live host set unconfirmed), ADR-0096 (renderer allowlist on every redirect hop, 'the class of F17 at another boundary'), ADR index row ADR-0069 'changed by ADR-0088', backlog 'Confirm fal's live media host set' (Product follow-ups), backlog 'First live run with real keys and real money' (says it settles fal's live media host set; leave C5 until its row is settled), status page docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F17
- **Tests:** packages/gateway/test/video.test.ts 'gateway: provider media admission (ADR-0088)': 'refuses a clip at %s without fetching it, and still ledgers the billed clip' (15 URLs: 127.0.0.1, [::1], 2130706433, 0x7f000001, 169.254.169.254, 10.0.0.1, localhost., 127.0.0.1.nip.io, v3.fal.media., :8443, userinfo, suffix trick, evilfal.media, example.com, http://); 'refuses a last frame outside the media host the same way, keeping the clip'; 'refuses a redirect from the media host to %s without following it' (same list plus http://127.0.0.1:1234/admin); 'refuses a redirect that names no address'; 'follows redirects within the media host, absolute or relative, every request under redirect "manual" and one deadline' (also asserts wire.media origins); 'follows at most three redirects'; 'reports a media host status as a media failure: a CDN 401 is not a missing key, and only 408, 429 and 5xx are tried again'; 'stops reading at the byte cap when no length is declared, cancelling the body, and still ledgers the clip' (201 MiB lazy countingBody); 'refuses a declared length over the cap before reading anything'; 'types the clip from its bytes...'; video.test.ts 'fal media helpers': admitMediaUrl, readCapped ('grows past a declared length that was too small, and stops at the cap'; 'keeps no chunk alive once it is copied' x2), sniffVideoType; plus 'refuses a download that is not https, or that the media host fails to serve, as a media failure (ADR-0088)'; packages/runner/test/pages.test.ts:415 'holds the allowlist on every redirect hop...' (renderer, ADR-0096); packages/runner/test/activities.test.ts 'stops the step when the image was billed but byway could not keep it...' (asset re-buy); Ran in clone (8 files, 226 tests pass). Mutations (each reverted): hop admission removed -> 16 video tests fail; redirect 'follow' -> 1 fails; cap check removed -> 2 fail; readCapped replaced by arrayBuffer -> 1 fails; media status thrown as a plain HTTP error -> 2 fail; byte sniff replaced by 'video/mp4' -> 2 fail

On main admitMediaUrl rejects any IP literal or non-fal host before fetch (fal.ts:215). #### F18: F18 — Enable a real browser-process containment boundary (byway-review.md:141 summary row, :339-347 body, :378 threat table, :427 order; produced by engine-protocol-review.md E9, :114-122)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT (both verifiers and both critics); severity kept at P2. Reframed: key custody is an extension of ADR-0030's 'keys never enter a sandbox', not a breach of spec §2 / ADR-0074 'Keys' (verification 3859, 4137).; severity P2. (Verification: 45 (§1 verdict row), 68 (§2 cluster H row), 83 (§3 adversarial row), 90 (§4 D2), 3820-3834 (cluster root cause), 3837-3871 (F18 verdict, rationale, severity, root cause), 3874-3951 (options A-D), 3954-4096 (recommendation B: design, steps, acceptance tests, compatibility, ADR, not doing), 4098-4120 (how it resolves F18), 4123-4157 (changes forced by the adversarial rounds), 4160-4166 (final check residuals). Review: byway-review.md 141, 339-347, 378, 427; engine-protocol-review.md 114-122.)
- **Root cause:** launchChromium (pages.ts:241-243 at b33f128), the only production launch every renderer use passes through, stated no containment policy, so two defaults built for other contexts applied: Playwright's chromiumSandbox:false (adds --no-sandbox) and inheritance of process.env, which serve.ts had loaded with .env.agents' provider keys. The product described renderer containment only as in-browser controls (spec §3.2, ADR-0074) after ADR-0067 dropped ADR-0031's OS-isolated renderer without replacing it, and treated the renderer as a web-page renderer although BLIND-1 made it the shared renderer for every medium's lineup cards. No test checked effective flags or environment, and the lazy launch meant a renderer that cannot start was found only after paid steps (verification 3820-3834, 3865-3871).
- **Wider than reviewed:** (1) The renderer inherits the provider keys from .env.agents. (2) It also decodes user-uploaded reference images (checked only by magic bytes) and provider images on lineup cards for every medium including the C5 gate, a delivery path needing no model cooperation (verification 45, 3847-3852, 4124-4130). (3) Lazy launch: a non-sandboxable host fails only after paid steps; Playwright's raw text advises chromiumSandbox:false and Temporal retries it (3831-3832, 4131-4134). (4) launchChromium(extraArgs) let any caller re-add --no-sandbox (3934, 4135). (5) On Windows the broker and network-service utility stay at Medium integrity even sandboxed (3916, 4138). (6) Linux with restricted userns, root and containers would stop rendering (platform trade, owner decision D2) (3917, 4141). (7) Env scrub is a deny-list (3919, 4136). (8) Playwright pinned at 1.63.0 accumulates n-days; CDN allowlist admits any npm package; protocol/06-runbook.md RENDER snippet is unsandboxed (4093-4094, 4143). Final check residuals (4162-4165): other-media card lineups reach the renderer only after spend; names-from-file scrub would also change next build (HTTPS_PROXY, BYWAY_NEXT_DIST_DIR); with the pipe transport the first line is 'Target page, context or browser has been closed'; apps/web/README.md:91 and the e2e start-up line not asserted.
- **Refuted or overstated:** Nothing in the review's core was refuted. The verification judged the review's 'narrow artifact interface' unnecessary (the route answers only exact recorded sha256 ids validated by the store, behind the egress allowlist; 4091, 4144), and judged a same-user helper process to add little over Chromium's broker/renderer split, with a separate OS identity disproportionate for a local single-user app (3889-3891, 4082) – declined rather than refuted. It also corrected its own first design's claim that a sandbox failure is 'the same as a missing Chromium' (4131).
- **Resolution (decided and built):** Both halves of the root cause were removed at the one launch boundary. chromiumLaunchOptions(setting, env) (packages/runner/src/pages.ts:315-316) is the only description of the launch: headless, chromiumSandbox from the person's setting, args exactly the two WebRTC flags, env = withoutSecrets(env, namesFromEnvFile(env)). launchChromium(setting) (pages.ts:2298-2306) is the only production launch, takes only the sandbox setting, calls Playwright once and maps any failure through launchFailure (pages.ts:348) with no retry and no fallback. The setting defaults on (RENDERER_OS_SANDBOX_DEFAULT = true; store's renderer_settings row, never set = on; an unreadable setting counts as on; serve.ts:191 also defaults on). Only Settings' PATCH /api/renderer writes it, and apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts pins every line that switches the setting, sets chromiumSandbox, builds a renderer or launches Chromium. A sandbox that cannot start gives per-OS why and fix (packages/runner/src/sandbox.ts), read from the whole error so Playwright's pipe-transport first line does not hide it; a drift test holds the words to the installed Playwright. Readiness reaches the sandboxed renderer process: ready() draws byway's own scripted test page (pages.ts:732-765); beginRun gates web-page runs (lifecycle.ts:28) and the model step gates runs with >=3 references including an image (model-step.ts:189-197, 890f88f); serve.ts prints a 'renderer' line and records it for Settings and the banner; scripts/e2e.ts:141-146 fails unless the line says 'inside its OS sandbox'. While the sandbox is off: confirmation, banner on every screen, a run note per step, the start-up line, and an offer to turn it back on. CI runs the whole gate on windows-2025 and ubuntu-24.04 (after scripts/linux/allow-chromium-sandbox.sh) with the sandbox on (run 39172144829 on the branch; main 8401820 run 39177268735 green). I confirmed in my clone that the targeted tests pass (launch, begin-run, sandbox, render-browsers, sandbox-note, gateway env, store renderer-settings, guard-sandbox: 104/104; renderer-check 21/21; the four real-Chromium pages.test.ts sandbox tests), and I ran five mutations, each caught: chromiumSandbox forced false (5 tests fail, including the CDP command-line test), env dropped from the options (2 fail), an unsandboxed retry inside launchChromium (6 fail), the beginRun gate removed (2 fail), and a stray chromium.launch in packages/runner/src (3 guard tests fail). One known exception to the env half stays open: the secrets-file names that do not look secret leak when the gateway's env module loads twice on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6). Provider keys and secret-looking names still stay out, because those rules go by name.
- **Records:** ADR-0084 (renderer launches with no secrets, one stated policy; sandbox awaited D2) and its later notes of 2026-10-18, ADR-0105 (OS sandbox on by default; only the person switches it off, in Settings; D2), ADR-0106 (a late close retires the renderer's browser; enables setting changes without failing loads in flight), ADR-0100 items 5 and 6 (other-media readiness taken; lowercase-drive exception to the secrets-file-name scrub), ADR-0090 'Readiness before spend for card lineups of other media', ADR-0103 §3 (CI on Windows and Linux; changed by 0105/0106), docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F18 and Owner decisions bullet, README.md 'Renderer' / 'The OS sandbox' sections; spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-product-v1-design.md §2 Keys (line 42) and render row (line 72); apps/web/README.md:91, :146-149, docs/backlog.md rows: 'What D2 left (ADR-0105)', 'What the late-close fix left (ADR-0106)', 'Secrets-file names independent of module identity', 'Playwright bump policy', 'Windows: Chromium's network service runs at Medium integrity', 'Renderer environment as an allowlist', 'Renderer egress: narrow the CDN allowlist', 'Protocol runbook's RENDER snippet', 'macOS support', 'A hosted byway', 'Pin pages.shownText's launch failure in launch.test.ts'
- **Tests:** packages/runner/test/pages.test.ts 'starts Chromium from the one stated launch: inside its OS sandbox unless the setting is off, with the WebRTC flags, as the command line shows (F18; ADR-0105)' (real Chromium, CDP, negative control); packages/runner/test/pages.test.ts 'when asked whether it is ready, draws byway's own test page once on each browser...'; 'reads the OS sandbox setting before each load...'; 'fails plainly when the page renderer cannot draw byway's test page...'; packages/runner/test/launch.test.ts 'is headless, inside the OS sandbox or not as the setting says, adds only the WebRTC flags, and hands Chromium no key or secrets-file name'; 'takes only the sandbox setting in production and calls Playwright once...'; 'is on unless the person switched it off...'; 'comes out of the production launch as that failure, after one attempt...'; 'on every renderer path' (view, card, readiness check, lineup of cards); packages/runner/test/sandbox.test.ts (per-OS reasons, never advising sandbox off, drift test against the installed Playwright); packages/runner/test/begin-run.test.ts 'refuses a web-page run whose page renderer cannot start, plainly, not retried, before anything is spent'; 'a model step whose run has image references' > 'stops before any provider call when its renderer cannot start'; packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts (start-up check, probe limits, line; run refusal agrees with start-up line); packages/runner/test/render-browsers.test.ts 'a setting that cannot be read counts as on: byway never draws without the sandbox by mistake' and the retirement tests; packages/runner/test/sandbox-note.test.ts; packages/store/test/renderer-settings.test.ts 'are the sandbox on, with no check, when never set'; packages/gateway/test/env.test.ts withoutSecrets / namesFromEnvFile; apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts; apps/web/test/renderer.test.ts; apps/web/e2e/renderer-sandbox.spec.ts; scripts/e2e.ts start-up line assertion; scripts/test/ci.test.ts; scripts/test/serve.test.ts

**Gaps:**

- The status page row for F18 ('Fixed'; 'What remains': macOS, AppArmor tested on CI only) is true as far as it goes but incomplete. It leaves out the open ADR-0100 item 6 exception to 'no name from .env.agents in Chromium's environment' (lowercase-drive double module load), which README and spec §2 still state as an exception. It also leaves out the other F18 residual rows (Medium-integrity network service, CDN breadth, deny-list env, Playwright bump cadence, protocol runbook). Its Records column omits ADR-0100, ADR-0090 and ADR-0106.
- The lowercase-drive secrets-file-name leak has no regression test and no trigger. Its mechanism (env.ts:96 module-level WeakMap keyed by module identity) is unchanged on main, so that sub-part is filed, not fixed.
- Several residual rows are to-dos with no condition that brings them back: 'Windows: Chromium's network service runs at Medium integrity', 'Renderer egress: narrow the CDN allowlist', the cadence part of 'Playwright bump policy', 'Pin pages.shownText's launch failure in launch.test.ts', and 'What D2 left' items (2), (3), (7), (8), (9).
- Deviation from the verification's chosen design, recorded rather than hidden: B's 'never unsandboxed' and 'no opt-out in v1' were superseded by the owner's explicit Settings switch (D2). A start-up probe chosen by Claude and kept by the owner draws byway's own test page without the sandbox to diagnose a failure. Untrusted run content is never drawn unsandboxed unless the person switched the sandbox off.

#### F19: F19: Reject invalid Unicode in canonical object keys (byway-review.md:349-355; sub-review gateway-store-review.md GS-07, lines 29 and 99-103)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT (verification line 46, 4176). Reproduced at b33f1285 by both verifiers and the designer's probes.; severity P3 (review P3). Lines 4180: the library breaks RFC 8785 and its own contract. Every path to the defect needs a lone surrogate, which can only arrive as a JSON \u escape.. (Verdict table 46; cluster table 69; cluster root cause 4171; F19 4174-4182; options O1-O6 4185-4276; recommendation, design, steps and acceptance 4278-4316; compatibility 4319; ADR 4321; not doing 4324-4335; how it resolves 4338-4339; adversarial changes 4342-4354; final check and residuals 4357-4362)
- **Root cause:** Lines 4171 and 4182. Well-formed text is a precondition of hashing (RFC 8785 for canonical JSON, UTF-8 for sha256Hex), but only canonical's string-value branch enforced it. Commit 54eed9e added the check there alone, so property names bypassed it, and sha256Hex silently hashed a lone surrogate as U+FFFD (a collision with the U+FFFD text). Deeper cause: none of the three places where untrusted text enters a hashed record made sure it was hashable. (1) parseRecipe returned recipes that recipeHash could not hash. (2) Ledger.verify assumed every parsed line was a hashable entry object. (3) ModelGateway.record copied provider-reported labels into the hash-chained entry after billing.
- **Wider than reviewed:** (a) sha256Hex has the same flaw: sha256Hex('a\ud800b') === sha256Hex('a�b'). (b) The review's one-line repair alone would turn three clean outcomes into uncaught throws: the composer's COMPOSED-KNOB, `byway hash` exiting 0, and verify() naming a tampered key (4199). (c) parseRecipe made unhashable recipes, so the composer, `byway hash` and runner.start already crashed on a lone-surrogate value or title. (d) Ledger.verify threw on a lone-surrogate value, on 1e400 and on a null line. Its first-round fix let a line with no hash field pass (4343). (e) The provider-label boundary: a billed call whose model id held \ud800 raised billed-not-ledgered with spend 0 (ENG-3, 4178, 4344). (f) Handed off or parked: non-finite usage went to F03, and the damaged .head file went to the backlog (4296, 4330-4331, 4353). (g) Final-check residuals (4359-4362): verify() throws a RangeError on a deeply nested line; non-string provider labels passed through unnormalised; an acceptance-test wording slip (ST7 versus ST3); zod's 'Unrecognized key' message echoes a raw lone surrogate.
- **Refuted or overstated:** The refuter's two corrections were kept (4178). (a) 'Interoperability' is theoretical, because the product has no second canonicalizer. (b) The review's repair alone is not a safe fix: it regresses callers that rely on 'what I parsed can be hashed' (O1 cons, 4199). The underlying defect claim was not refuted.
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** The library now conforms. packages/canonical/src/index.ts:22-25 has one serializeString, used by values (:32) and by property names (:64), and the name is checked before its value is walked. sha256Hex (:78-80) throws a CanonicalizationError on ill-formed text. Each untrusted boundary now guarantees hashability by construction:
- parseRecipe (packages/recipe/src/schema.ts:288-305) calls recipeHash and turns a CanonicalizationError into one ZodError issue at the recipe root. compose.ts:118, cli main.ts:37 and runner client.ts:105 (before createRun) all go through it, and none of them catches.
- Ledger.verify (packages/gateway/src/ledger.ts:66-73 expectedHash; :534 non-object guard; :537-541 explicit expected===undefined) reports damage instead of throwing.
- ModelGateway.record (gateway.ts:903-911, providerLabel :943-946) is the single funnel for all three paid kinds (the only callers are :275, :379 and :589). It writes string labels with toWellFormed() and non-string labels as their JSON text.

Later work kept the rule. The 48800d2 merge returns the same well-formed label to callers (ADR-0100 item 1). The ADR-0102 decline reasons go through cleanReason, which also uses toWellFormed (declines.ts:27). The F03 work made usage finite (finiteUsage, 4c02821) and made verify() report a damaged head as 'head' (2db4435).

I ran the tests in my clone at 8401820: 8 files, 289 tests passed (canonical, recipe, gateway ledger and gateway, advisor compose-unicode, cli, validator review-regressions, catalog conformance). I then removed each guard in turn and every removal was caught:
- keys back to bare JSON.stringify: 10 failures in 4 files;
- sha256Hex check removed: 1 failure;
- parseRecipe hash check removed: 11 failures across recipe, advisor and cli;
- verify judged by hash equality: 2 failures;
- verify without the non-object guard: 1 failure (the null case);
- providerLabel returning the raw string: 2 failures.
- **Records:** ADR-0083 (Accepted; Decision 1-5; Consequences; 'Not covered here'), changed by ADR-0100 items 1-4, ADR-0091 decision 5 (verify reports a damaged head as 'head'; closes ADR-0083's .head gap), ADR-0095 decision 7 (non-finite usage or cost settled at planning figures, flagged assumed; closes the F03 hand-off), ADR-0088 (scrubLabels after providerLabel; the caller gets the ledger's label), docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-recipe-core-design.md:31, :33, :172 (canonical section updated in 888d525), docs/backlog.md 'Ledger.verify throws on a deeply nested line' (line 163), docs/backlog.md 'Escape names in reserved-key violation paths' (line 127), docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F19 (line 29): Fixed, 0083, remains '—'
- **Tests:** packages/canonical/test/canonical.test.ts: 'rejects %s in a property name, naming it escaped in a well-formed message' (root high, root low, nested low, reversed pair); 'checks a property name before its value, so the outer bad name is the one reported'; 'keeps the message of a lone surrogate in a value as it was'; 'serializes well-formed property names exactly as before' (astral, U+0000, U+FFFF, U+2028, literal backslash-u, decomposed e; bytes checked); 'sorts object keys by UTF-16 code units' (RFC §3.2.3 vector); sha256Hex 'refuses text with a lone surrogate, which has no UTF-8 encoding (F19)'; 'hashes the UTF-8 bytes' (abc vector); packages/recipe/test/recipe.test.ts: 'parseRecipe: every recipe it returns can be hashed (F19)': 'refuses a lone surrogate in %s with one well-formed issue naming where it is'; 'either refuses an adversarial input with a ZodError or returns a recipe that hashes'; 'hashes every stored catalog recipe exactly as before once read back through parseRecipe'; packages/advisor/test/compose-unicode.test.ts: 'reports one in %s as a SCHEMA violation at the recipe, with no recipe and no throw'; 'repairs such an answer once and offers the repaired recipe'; packages/cli/test/cli.test.ts: 'a lone surrogate in a recipe file (F19)': 'in %s: validate and hash both exit 2 with "is not a valid recipe"'; packages/gateway/test/ledger.test.ts: 'Ledger.verify on lines that cannot be hashed' (value, key, removed hash on the last and first line, null/array/string/number lines, costUsd 1e400); 'Ledger.verify on a damaged head record'; packages/gateway/test/gateway.test.ts: 'gateway: malformed provider labels still reach the ledger' (served model, upstream host, lone-surrogate response key, non-string served model); packages/gateway/test/spend-book.test.ts: 'provider-reported usage that is not a finite number (ADR-0083's hand-off)'; packages/validator/test/review-regressions.test.ts: 'a NaN count is reported, not thrown' (ST3 guard); packages/catalog/test/conformance.test.ts (recipes/ and manifest unchanged); packages/runner/test/build.test.ts (bundle-hash pin)

**Gaps:**

- The status page row F19 (2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:29) says 'What remains: —'. That is not quite true: two backlog rows still trace to ADR-0083 (the deep-nesting RangeError in verify, and escaping names in reserved-key violation paths), and ADR-0083 accepts named limits (the zod echo, U+FFFD labels, order-dependent fallback hashes). F03's row, by contrast, says 'Named residuals in the backlog'.
- The backlog row 'Escape names in reserved-key violation paths' gives no trigger that would bring it back.
- Not verified: ADR-0083 calls the zod 'Unrecognized key' echo cosmetic because 'every consumer serializes violations as escaped JSON'. One consumer is a real provider receiving the repair prompt: it decodes the \ud800 escape and may reject the request. Whether it does was not checked, because paid calls are not allowed. The demo-path test asserts well-formedness only for the title case.
- Minor, not filed: the F02 shape bound (packages/recipe/src/shape.ts:62-82) also builds issue paths from raw keys. A recipe that goes over the depth or node limit beneath a lone-surrogate name would carry a raw surrogate in its path, the same cosmetic class as the reserved-key row, which names only reservedKeyPaths.
- Minor: runner.start has no test of its own for refusing a lone-surrogate recipe before a run exists. The guarantee rests on parseRecipe's tests and on the call order in client.ts:105-116.

#### F20: F20 — A work cap must not substitute for human acceptance (byway-review.md lines 143, 357-365; engine-protocol-review.md E8)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity P2 (review P2; low P2). (47, 61, 217-233, 340, 342, 382, 406-408, 438-439, 481, 528-530)
- **Root cause:** Gate-exit policy has no single definition. The interpreter gives human-metric loops a private exit branch that treats exhaustion like approval and never reads the declared onCap. The shared atCap handler is reached only when a model critic or check reports straight to the loop. Which critic feeds a gate is undecided: the validator's containsCritic walks through every container, while the interpreter drops the loop context at replicate, each, audit and gate. LS1 checks a declared onCap that one branch, and every feedless critic loop, ignore. Spec, protocol, estimator, catalog comment and validator each state a different exit policy (line 233).
- **Wider than reviewed:** onCap:'human' is dead configuration for C2 vision (max 3), C6a vision (max 2) and the C6/C6a author loop (max 10). LS1 certifies the author loop only because it contains a critic lens. Critic and check loops whose gate never sees the critic (variants3) also leave at the cap through bestOf on an empty history, without asking. The estimator is a third reading. No test drove a human loop to its cap (lines 223-227).
- **Refuted or overstated:** The consequence is overstated: every capped human loop in the catalog is followed by an always-run human VOICE, and the C2/C6a bounds are stated in the copy. The real losses are no raise-once option, no notice of the last round, C2's round-3 brief and C6/C6a's round-10 notes never shown at a decision, and in composed recipes a critic loop's cap standing in for the person (line 229).
- **Resolution (root cause fixed):** The private human-loop exit is gone. Every way a loop reaches its cap goes through Interpreter.capTurn (interpreter.ts:679-696): after a critic or check (atCap), at the last round of a person's loop (interpreter.ts:579-584, in place of the last 'Is this ready?'), and after a round that ran to its end with no verdict (:590-594). With onCap human, a person's loop asks 'That was the last planned round. Use this version? No allows up to N more rounds, once.' with the work and the round's notes beside it. No raises the cap once, and at the raised cap a 'choose' asks 'Use this version' or 'Stop the run' (RunStopped, which ends the run cancelled). Admission keeps the policy reachable: LS1 refuses a person's loop whose cap goes to a policy, ST2/ST10 'feed' refuse a critic or check loop or gate whose critic does not report on every round (gateFeed in the shared semantics), and ST10 'metric' refuses one-shot check-clean and human gates. The estimator (planned) and workBound (withRaise) read one loopRounds, and the C2/C6a copy says more rounds can be allowed once. Unit tests drive the real Interpreter to the cap, an e2e drives C6a's author loop through the real workflow, and semantics-exits holds the feed and exit semantics to the interpreter.
- **Records:** ADR-0093 (one cap policy; end-status policy; admission keeps it reachable; estimate; copy; the 'unchecked' exit is not a cap exit), ADR-0092 decision 1 (exit points, gateFeed), ADR-0098 decision 7 (LS1 names a person's loop first), docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md row F20
- **Tests:** packages/runner/test/loop-cap.test.ts 'humanGate(1): the cap question is asked, and Yes keeps the version'; 'humanGate(3), No every time: Is this ready? twice, the cap question, rounds 4 to 6, then the last choice'; ''Stop the run' at the last choice stops the run'; 'the cap question shows the work and the round's notes'; 'a critic loop at its cap ... whose gate never sees its critic ... asks Use the best version so far?'; packages/runner/test/e2e/loop-cap.ts (run in configurations.test.ts): 'asks at the last round; No raises it once...', ''Stop the run' at the last choice ends the run cancelled', 'Cancel while the cap question is open ends the run cancelled and withdraws the question' (needs Temporal; not run here, CI per status page); packages/runner/test/semantics-exits.test.ts 'a loop whose gate cannot see its critic runs its body to the end'; packages/validator/test/operator-semantics.test.ts 'ST2 feed...', 'ST10 feed and metric...', 'LS1: a loop a person holds ends on their answer'; packages/advisor/test/estimate.test.ts; packages/recipe/test/limits.test.ts (loopRounds planned/withRaise); Ran in clone: loop-cap, semantics-exits, operator-semantics, estimate and limits all passed

This is the review probe's own shape (real Interpreter, stubbed activities). |
| Centralize gate-exit policy; never treat exhaustion as approval | root-cause-fixed | One capTurn for atCap, the human branch and the fallthrough; ADR-0093 decision 1; a policy cap ends the loop only where declared, and LS1 refuses that on a person's loop. |

#### R1: Make verification repeatable in CI (release action 1)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity P3. (48 (verdict row), 70 (cluster table), 85, 92 (D4), 4392-4414 (finding), 4641-4657 (design item 1), 4768 (step 1), 4787 (acceptance), 4806, 4810, 4822-4828, 4842, 4871-4872, 4880-4882)
- **Root cause:** Verification exists only as documented local commands, and nothing runs them on change, so every 'CI check' the spec and the ADRs rely on is enforced from memory. The commands' prerequisites (Node, pnpm, runner OS, Chromium for the unit suite, the Temporal CLI) are written as minimums or manual steps, not provisioned, pinned inputs (verification:4414).
- **Wider than reviewed:** pnpm test itself needs provisioned tools: pages.test.ts and cards.test.ts launch real Chromium, and the harness downloads the Temporal CLI, so README:95's 'no network' line was wrong. The repo had never run on Linux, so the POSIX branches (dev-server.real.test.ts ps/lsof, serve.ts, e2e.ts) had never been exercised. Four accepted documents assume a CI that did not exist: spec :145/:170, ADR-0059 'Now', ADR-0057 CR14 and ADR-0068's merge gate (verification:4396-4405).
- **Refuted or overstated:** Dependencies were already pinned by pnpm-lock.yaml with a frozen install; only Node, pnpm, runner images, Chromium and the Temporal CLI were unpinned. 'No lint' is literally true, but strict tsconfig plus the guard tests are the spec's linters. The teardown diagnostics are intended: GracefulShutdownPeriodExpiredError is ENG-10's expected outcome (worker.ts:103-119) (verification:4407-4410).
- **Resolution (decided and built):** The cause is removed for main. .github/workflows/verify.yml runs `pnpm verify` (typecheck && vitest && e2e) on windows-2025 and ubuntu-24.04 on every push to main, by hand and weekly. It checks out the whole history, uses a read-only token and no secrets. Node is pinned in .node-version (24.13.1), pnpm in packageManager (pnpm@11.28.6), every action by commit SHA and runner images by explicit label. Chromium (with --with-deps on Linux) and the Temporal CLI are provisioned before the tests. Linux also needs an AppArmor profile and Noto CJK; no product code needed changing (ADR-0103 §3). scripts/test/ci.test.ts holds the workflow to this shape, so deleting it, changing its triggers or unpinning it fails `pnpm verify`. ADR-0103 §5-§6 restate what 'CI' means in CR14, ADR-0055, ADR-0059, the spec and ADR-0068's merge gate. The 'Protect main' and 'Protect tags' rulesets block force pushes and deletions; I read them as active with a read-only `gh api` call. CI blocks nothing: it reports after a push, and branches run CI only when dispatched by hand. A required check waits for a filed trigger. CI is green on main at 8401820 (run 39177268735). Main run 39079760987 went red, so the gate does fail.
- **Records:** ADR-0103 (§2 check, §3 CI, §4 platforms, §5 what 'CI' means in older records, §6 ADR-0068 merge gate restated, §8 rulesets, §9 when a blocking check comes), docs/adr/README.md: ADR-0055, ADR-0057, ADR-0059 and ADR-0068 'changed by ADR-0103', README Tests, CI and Scripts sections, spec §4 and §6 (name the guard tests CI runs), docs/playbook.md row 42 'Public record for the charter' (rulesets and ADR check in CI), packages/runner/test/liveness.test.ts ENG-10 comment
- **Tests:** scripts/test/ci.test.ts: 'runs `pnpm verify` itself, so it checks exactly what a person runs before merging'; 'runs on pushes to main, by hand and weekly, and on no other branch'; 'has one job per operating system: Windows and Linux, on pinned runner images'; 'pins Node in .node-version and pnpm in packageManager, and uses both'; 'provisions Chromium (with its Linux libraries) and the Temporal CLI before the tests'; 'pins every action and reusable workflow it uses to a commit...'; 'checks out the whole history...'; README platform/CI cases; 'branch and tag protection, as set on GitHub' (text checks only); scripts/test/readme.test.ts: 'says what the unit run needs instead of promising no network'; '`pnpm verify` runs the typecheck, the unit tests and the end-to-end suite, in that order...'; Ran in my clone (8401820): every file under scripts/test passed

|---|---|---|

- The verification's acceptance probe (a throwaway branch adding 'proven' to an apps/web string, expected to turn CI red via guard-claims, verification:4768/4787) was not run as designed. That CI can go red is shown instead by real red runs: the bring-up runs, and main run 39079760987.

#### R2: Record third-party licence decisions before distributing a packaged build (release action 2)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT_PARTLY; severity none. (49 (verdict row), 4417-4441, 4748-4757, 4783 (step 9), 4829, 4843-4850)
- **Root cause:** No step derives licence and notice obligations from what is actually distributed, because no distribution step exists yet. npm metadata alone also understates bundled native libraries and misses binaries downloaded at runtime (verification:4441).
- **Wider than reviewed:** The inventory misses caniuse-lite (CC-BY-4.0, a production dependency), the LGPL and MPL-1.1 libraries bundled inside sharp's binary, the Temporal CLI and Playwright Chromium downloaded at runtime, and the missing root LICENSE although package.json says 'Apache-2.0' (verification:4431-4435).
- **Refuted or overstated:** The framing over-includes: lightningcss was dev-only (pnpm why --prod empty), and sharp was only next@16.3.8's lazily loaded optional image optimizer, with next/image never imported. Nothing is distributed: the repo is private (ADR-0060) and install is from source (verification:4427-4437).
- **Resolution (filed with trigger):** There was nothing to fix in code. docs/backlog.md 'Before any distribution' records every verified fact and the required step: generate the licence and notice manifest from the actual artifact, not from npm metadata. Later checks updated two of the verification's facts honestly. Since the pnpm 11 lockfile (ADR-0103), sharp and lightningcss are also optional peers of minimizer-webpack-plugin in a production install (lockfile line 4124). Neither loads through it, because Temporal's bundler runs webpack in development mode. sharp can load at runtime through Next's live /_next/image endpoint, since next.config.ts leaves image optimization on (aa44400). The section also carries ADR-0059's two missing release CIs, and names a Claude Code or Codex plugin that others install as a distribution.
- **Records:** docs/backlog.md section 'Before any distribution', ADR-0103 §5 (ADR-0059 release CIs do not exist; no release process yet)
- **Tests:** none (no tripwire test; the trigger is the backlog section)

#### R3: Keep a compact current-decision index (release action 3)

- **Verification:** ACCEPT; severity P3. (50 (verdict row), 92 (D4), 4444-4462, 4659-4698 (design items 2-3), 4769-4770, 4788-4790, 4809, 4812-4817, 4851-4856, 4873-4874, 4885, 4893-4894)
- **Root cause:** The ADR log has a rule for replacing decisions (write a new ADR) but no structured, checked relation between a record and the records that change it. It also had no rule for finalising a Proposed record's Decision on acceptance, and CR14's append-only check was not implemented. So corrections land only in the new record, old text keeps reading as current, prose relation verbs are optional and inconsistent, and hand-maintained summaries carry no link to their source (verification:4462).
- **Wider than reviewed:** (a) ADR-0080 corrects, replaces and extends ADR-0074, 0072 and 0079, and none of them links back. (b) ADR-0077 and ADR-0079 change ADR-0071 with no back-link and no relation verb, and product ADRs 0064-0080 have no Related row. (c) Root README:110's 'ADR-0064 to ADR-0072' range was stale. (d) The seed claim also appears at protocol/04:200, 04:221 and README:130. CR14's guarantee had never been tested: the 73 Accepted records were unedited only because nobody had edited them (verification:4452-4458).
- **Refuted or overstated:** None of the claims. The index status columns were accurate for all 80 records. Option A's 'concise current-status map' was rejected as one more hand-maintained summary that recreates the root cause (4549, 4837).
- **Resolution (partly):** Built: ADR-0103 §1 (Context and Decision final once on main's first-parent history as Accepted; --no-ff merges; the accepting commit may finalise; from ADR-0103 on, the 'changed by' link lands in the same commit) and §2. scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts with adr-log.ts runs in pnpm verify and in CI. It fails an edit to, or the removal of, an Accepted record's Context or Decision after it reached main; an Accepted Decision that opens 'Proposed'; an index status that differs from the header; a 'changed by' link to a missing or later record or one not reciprocated; a record with no index row; and, from ADR-0103 on, a late link. Its temp-repo cases prove the rule. ADR-0041 gets a non-ratification note in ADR-0103 §7. f230909 back-filled the index 'changed by' links, so 0071, 0072, 0074, 0079 and 0066 all point to the records that change them. Root README's stale range became a link (f6e4ca9), and readme.test.ts forbids such a range. Not built: the verification's named root cause, a structured and checked relation, is only partly removed. The links are hand-added to the index, and the check 'cannot see a missing "changed by" link' (ADR-0103 §2). A future record that corrects an old one without adding the link passes, which is how 0077 and 0079 drifted. The history-table Outcome pointers are not done.
- **Records:** ADR-0103 §1, §2, §7, §10, ADR-0094 (corrects ADR-0066), ADR-0100 (corrections by addition after the final re-check), docs/adr/README.md 'How to read this log' and 'changed by' links, backlog 'Structured "Changes" and "Later records" rows in ADRs, with a back-fill', backlog 'R3 remainder: outcome pointers for decisions already taken', playbook row 44 (confirm ADR-0041's rules; ratifications)
- **Tests:** scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts: 'no Accepted record’s Context or Decision changed after it reached main, except the edits named here'; 'each named exception matches an edit in main’s history'; 'from ADR-0103 on, a record that changes another reaches main in the same commit as the index's "changed by" link...'; 'an Accepted record’s Decision does not open as a proposal, except the records named here...'; 'each index row links its record, and its status is the record’s'; 'each "changed by" link names a record that reached main no earlier, and that record names the one it changes'; 'each record that has reached main has an index row'; temp-repo cases 'an edited Decision fails...', 'a removed record fails...', 'a draft revised on its branch passes when ... --no-ff, and fails when it is fast-forwarded'; scripts/test/readme.test.ts: 'names no hand-maintained range for the product decisions, and links the index sections that list them'; Ran in my clone: pass
